&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The table shows recommended starting metrics for each scenario. For the complete list of supported metrics, see the &lt;a href="https://awslabs.github.io/uaef/Guides/metrics-catalog/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;metrics catalog&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Pass the metrics you picked as the &lt;code&gt;metrics&lt;/code&gt; argument to &lt;code&gt;evaluate()&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;batch_evaluate()&lt;/code&gt;. For a worked example, see the Example notebooks section that follows. Deterministic metrics, such as tool calling and performance, are computed directly from the trace, whereas others call an LLM judge. UAEF automatically skips metrics that need a reference answer when no ground truth is present, so partially labeled datasets and unlabeled production traffic are still worth evaluating.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="choosing-the-pipeline"&gt;Choosing the pipeline&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Metrics decide what you measure. Six independent choices decide how the evaluation runs. Use the question in each row to settle it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Choice&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Ask yourself&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Then use&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;In code&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Passive or active&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Do I already have traces, or do I need to generate them under controlled conditions?&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Passive:&lt;/strong&gt; evaluate a trace you already have. &lt;strong&gt;Active:&lt;/strong&gt; call an invoke_* helper so UAEF runs the agent.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt; &lt;p&gt;evaluate(trace=recorded,&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;adapter=“langgraph”)&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;or&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;raw = invoke_http_agent_for_framework(&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;ENDPOINT, query, “strands”)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;One trace or a dataset&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Am I debugging one case or measuring a release?&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Use evaluate() for one trace or conversation. Use batch_evaluate() for a dataset.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt; &lt;p&gt;evaluate(trace=trace)&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;or&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;batch_evaluate(traces=traces,&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;max_workers=4)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Single-turn or multi-turn&lt;sup&gt;a&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Does any test case depend on an earlier turn?&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Multi-turn:&lt;/strong&gt; add session_id and turn_id to the dataset and pass every turn to the adapter, which assembles each conversation.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt; &lt;p&gt;sessions = adapter.transform_to_canonical(&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;turn_payloads)&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;batch_evaluate(traces=sessions)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Single-agent or multi-agent&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Does more than one agent handle a single request?&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Nothing to configure. The adapter returns an AgentTrace or MultiAgentTrace, and UAEF selects the matching evaluator.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt; &lt;p&gt;adapter = get_adapter(FRAMEWORK)&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;trace = adapter.transform_to_canonical(raw)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Offline or online&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Am I testing before release or monitoring production?&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Offline:&lt;/strong&gt; a prepared dataset in development and CI. &lt;strong&gt;Online:&lt;/strong&gt; live conversations, scored turn by turn.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt; &lt;p&gt;batch_evaluate(…)&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;or&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;OnlineEvaluator().evaluate_response(&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;trace=trace)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Ephemeral or persisted&lt;sup&gt;b&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Will I compare this run with a later one?&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Persisted:&lt;/strong&gt; set persist=True with an experiment name. Otherwise, use the returned result.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt; &lt;p&gt;batch_evaluate(traces=traces, persist=True,&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;experiment_name=“Support agent v2”)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;sup&gt;a&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Multi-turn –&lt;/strong&gt; For details, refer to the &lt;a href="https://awslabs.github.io/uaef/Advanced/multi-turn-evaluation-workflow/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;multi-turn evaluation workflow guide&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;sup&gt;b&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Only persisted runs can be compared&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="preparing-your-dataset"&gt;Preparing your dataset&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Provide one row per test case in a CSV or XLSX file. Only &lt;code&gt;Question&lt;/code&gt; is required.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Column&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Required&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Purpose&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Question&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Yes&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Input sent to the agent&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Answer&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;No&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Expected response&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;context&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;No&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Reference documents for retrieval-style evaluation&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;expected_tool_calls&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;No&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Expected tool calls as a JSON array&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;session_id&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Multi-turn only&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Groups rows into one conversation&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;turn_id&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Multi-turn only&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Orders turns within a conversation, starting at 1&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;Question&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;Answer&lt;/code&gt; are matched case-insensitively and accept common aliases such as &lt;code&gt;Query&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;Expected_Output&lt;/code&gt;, so existing test files usually work without renaming.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Multi-turn datasets.&lt;/strong&gt; Rows that share a &lt;code&gt;session_id&lt;/code&gt; are sent in order to a single agent session, so later turns can depend on earlier ones. Your agent endpoint must maintain its own conversation state, because UAEF sends each turn with its session ID rather than replaying history. Use lowercase &lt;code&gt;session_id&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;turn_id&lt;/code&gt;. Otherwise, the file is evaluated as single-turn.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Protect sensitive data.&lt;/strong&gt; Before sharing or persisting a dataset drawn from real traffic, use &lt;code&gt;uaef.security.PIIDetector&lt;/code&gt; to detect and redact personally identifiable information such as email addresses, phone numbers, and credit card numbers.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="example-notebooks"&gt;Example notebooks&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If you want to see more examples, check our &lt;a href="https://github.com/awslabs/uaef/tree/main/notebooks" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;notebooks/&lt;/a&gt; folder. The notebooks cover scenarios such as batch evaluation, multi-turn conversations, multi-agent, and experiment management across frameworks including LangGraph, Strands Agents, and Amazon Bedrock AgentCore.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Notebook&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What it covers&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;00_langgraph.ipynb&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;LangGraph: single, batch, multi-turn, and multi-agent evaluation&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;02_langchain.ipynb&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;LangChain agents&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;04_json.ipynb&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Custom frameworks, through the generic JSON adapter&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;05_strands.ipynb&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Strands Agents, including multi-agent&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;06_agentcore.ipynb&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, including multi-agent&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;07_langfuse.ipynb&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Traces already collected in Langfuse&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;01_langgraph_ragas_deepeval.ipynb&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;RAGAS and DeepEval integrations&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;08_experiment_management.ipynb&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Experiments, baselines, and regression detection&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;10_reporting_pipeline.ipynb&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Reports and dashboards from stored results&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;11_pii_detection.ipynb&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;PII detection and redaction in traces&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Running the library creates nothing on its own, but persisting a run writes experiment metadata to Amazon DynamoDB and full results to Amazon S3. Delete both when you no longer need the saved experiments. The default names are &lt;code&gt;uaef-experiments&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;uaef-results&lt;/code&gt;, overridable through the &lt;code&gt;UAEF_DYNAMODB_TABLE&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;UAEF_S3_BUCKET&lt;/code&gt; environment variables. The bucket has to be emptied before it can be deleted.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If you deployed the service, the &lt;a href="https://github.com/awslabs/uaef/blob/main/uaef-service/README.md" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;service deployment guide&lt;/a&gt; has the teardown sequence for both deployment modes. Read its retained-resources note before you assume teardown is complete: the DynamoDB tables, S3 buckets, and Amazon Cognito user pool are created with a retain policy, so deleting the stack leaves them, and their contents, in place. That is deliberate, so a teardown does not destroy saved experiments, but it means the storage keeps costing you until you remove it yourself.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;UAEF separates agent evaluation from the framework that produced the agent. Adapters convert traces from LangGraph, LangChain, Strands Agents, Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, and Langfuse into a common format, and a generic JSON adapter handles other frameworks. The same 36 built-in metrics across 7 dimensions then apply regardless of how a team built the agent. UAEF evaluates individual traces or runs batch evaluations over historical traces, then stores results per experiment in Amazon DynamoDB and Amazon S3. Teams can then compare runs across agent versions, detect regressions, and use the generated reports and insights to decide what to change in the next iteration. The web UI and the Python library share the same evaluation engine, so teams can start low-code and move to code without changing how scoring or storage works.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started, see the &lt;a href="https://github.com/awslabs/uaef" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;open source repository&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Introducing usage-based pricing for Amazon SageMaker AI bidirectional streaming</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/introducing-usage-based-pricing-for-amazon-sagemaker-ai-bidirectional-streaming/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Victor Wang]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 11 Nov 2026 15:10:23 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon SageMaker AI]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Customer Solutions]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">09f20b599e1ea4e477b4979d15326bbd992cf96d</guid>

					<description>AWS Marketplace now supports usage-based metering for Amazon SageMaker bidirectional streaming inference, so model providers can charge for what customers actually consume during a live stream. Deepgram is the launch partner, bringing usage-based speech-to-text and text-to-speech billing to AWS Marketplace.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Usage-based metering for bidirectional streaming solves a software billing problem for real-time speech AI. Customers deploying streaming models on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/introducing-bidirectional-streaming-for-real-time-inference-on-amazon-sagemaker-ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon SageMaker AI bidirectional streaming&lt;/a&gt; pay for the endpoint infrastructure that hosts the model. Separately, model providers listing on AWS Marketplace need a way to charge for what customers consume during a live stream. Today, we’re announcing usage-based metering for Amazon SageMaker bidirectional (bidi) streaming inference, so model providers can report actual usage for the AWS Marketplace software charge. Amazon SageMaker AI endpoint infrastructure charges still apply.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://deepgram.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Deepgram&lt;/a&gt;, the real-time speech AI company, is the launch partner for this capability, bringing usage-based Speech-to-Text (STT) and Text-to-Speech (TTS) billing to AWS Marketplace. This post builds on a series of innovations between Deepgram and AWS. First, we &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/introducing-bidirectional-streaming-for-real-time-inference-on-amazon-sagemaker-ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;introduced bidirectional streaming for real-time inference on Amazon SageMaker AI&lt;/a&gt;, which supports long-lived bidirectional streams between clients and model servers. Then, Deepgram &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/deepgram-enhances-amazon-sagemaker-ai-support-with-aws-iam-temporary-delegation/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;enhanced Amazon SageMaker AI support with AWS Identity and Access Management (AWS IAM) temporary delegation&lt;/a&gt;, simplifying secure authentication for enterprise deployments, and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/deepgram-deepens-amazon-sagemaker-ai-observability-with-enhanced-metrics/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;expanded observability with enhanced metrics&lt;/a&gt;. Now, with usage-based metering, we further strengthen the foundation for production real-time AI, aligning billing with how customers consume streaming and real-time AI workloads.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="why-streaming-broke-the-existing-billing-model"&gt;Why streaming broke the existing billing model&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With AWS Marketplace software metering for SageMaker AI real-time request-response inference, model providers can report a unit count in an HTTP response header. AWS Marketplace uses that quantity for the software charge. This works for request-response APIs where the billable quantity is known when the response is sent.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Bidirectional streaming is different. A client streams audio and receives transcriptions through the SageMaker AI API using an HTTP/2 event stream. SageMaker AI connects to the model server over WebSockets. A session can last 30 seconds or 30 minutes. Usage accumulates as the session runs, and the final total isn’t known until it ends. A response header alone can’t report periodic usage during that live session.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Hourly software pricing can charge customers while a model is available, including quiet periods. With usage-based metering, providers can align the AWS Marketplace software charge with reported usage. SageMaker AI endpoint infrastructure charges remain separate and continue to apply while the endpoint is provisioned.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="how-companion-metadata-streaming-works"&gt;How companion metadata streaming works&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The solution introduces a second WebSocket connection alongside the existing inference stream. When a bidirectional streaming session starts, SageMaker AI establishes two connections to the model server:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Data connection&lt;/strong&gt;: carries inference request and response data, the existing bidirectional stream.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Metadata connection&lt;/strong&gt;: a companion WebSocket dedicated to metering data.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Model servers opt in by returning one header on the WebSocket upgrade response:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;X-Amzn-SageMaker-Metadata-Stream-Supported: true&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;SageMaker AI then opens a companion WebSocket to the model server at &lt;code&gt;/invocations-bidirectional-stream-metadata&lt;/code&gt;. Both connections carry the same &lt;code&gt;X-Amzn-SageMaker-Request-Id&lt;/code&gt; header, which the model server uses to correlate metering records with the active inference session.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The model server sends metering data as JSON text frames on the metadata connection. Send records at a frequency that matches the billing model: incrementally as usage accrues (recommended), either as each unit is consumed or as periodic batches of units consumed since the previous record. Send any final record as soon as the session ends. Each frame contains exactly one metering record, rather than an array of records. The metadata stream has a rate quota of one message per second per connection, with a maximum message size of 512 bytes.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We recommend sending metering records incrementally as usage accrues. This reduces the amount of usage left unreported if a session ends unexpectedly. Transport delivery alone does not establish that usage has been billed. Validate reported units against AWS Marketplace reports and buyer charges.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
  "Metering": {
    "Dimension": "inference.count",
    "ConsumedUnits": 720,
    "ClientToken": "a3f2b1c0-unique-uuid"
  }
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Metering messages use the &lt;code&gt;inference.count&lt;/code&gt; dimension, and &lt;code&gt;ConsumedUnits&lt;/code&gt; supplies the quantity for that dimension. If the product charges per second of audio, a record reporting 12 minutes of metered audio carries 720 seconds as &lt;code&gt;ConsumedUnits: 720&lt;/code&gt;. The dimension name doesn’t mean one unit per request. The provider defines which audio counts as metered usage, and the AWS Marketplace offer maps the unit to its price. The listing shows one price per unit, labeled per request, with up to three decimal places. The &lt;code&gt;ClientToken&lt;/code&gt; field supports idempotency. If a duplicate token arrives on the same connection, SageMaker AI drops the duplicate message silently.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The metadata connection runs over TCP, which provides ordered, reliable delivery at the transport layer. If it fails to establish or drops mid-session, inference continues by default because &lt;code&gt;X-Amzn-SageMaker-Metadata-Stream-Required&lt;/code&gt; defaults to &lt;code&gt;false&lt;/code&gt;. Set this header to &lt;code&gt;true&lt;/code&gt; when the provider must stop inference if the metadata stream becomes unavailable. This choice trades inference availability for metering continuity. It doesn’t guarantee that malformed or dropped records are billed.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="what-this-means-for-customers"&gt;What this means for customers&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If you deploy real-time AI models for transcription, contact centers, voice agents, or captioning, the new metering model changes how the AWS Marketplace software charge is calculated. SageMaker AI endpoint infrastructure charges still apply.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Transparent, per-unit billing through AWS Marketplace means usage flows directly into your AWS bill. You get consolidated AWS billing, negotiated rates through private offers, and no separate billing relationship with the model provider to manage.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Pay for audio processed according to the product’s unit definition. If the product charges per second of audio, 12 minutes of metered audio equals 720 billable seconds and &lt;code&gt;ConsumedUnits: 720&lt;/code&gt;. An open connection lasting 12 minutes doesn’t, by itself, define the billable audio quantity. The provider’s counting rules determine the metered usage, whereas endpoint infrastructure charges remain separate.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Model providers can offer negotiated per-unit rates or fixed-price contracts through AWS Marketplace private offers.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We recommend discussing directly with your AWS account representative or partner representative.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="what-this-means-for-model-providers"&gt;What this means for model providers&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If you’re a model provider listing on AWS Marketplace, you can configure your model server to report usage during streaming inference sessions through companion metadata streaming.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-sagemaker-marketplace-onboarding-skill" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;SageMaker Marketplace Onboarding Agent Skill&lt;/a&gt; accelerates onboarding with an opt-in inference metering path. Its starter templates advertise metadata-stream support and build validated, incremental &lt;code&gt;ConsumedUnits&lt;/code&gt; frames for the companion WebSocket. Review and production-harden the code before submission.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Align billing with your pricing model by defining what one unit represents and reporting units as the session progresses. AWS Marketplace charges one per-unit price for real-time inference.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following three-step walkthrough describes how to integrate your model server to report metering data during bidirectional streaming inference sessions.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-1-opt-in-using-data-websocket-response-headers"&gt;Step 1: Opt in using data WebSocket response headers&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When SageMaker AI opens the data WebSocket connection to your model server, include these headers in your WebSocket upgrade (101) response:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Header&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Required&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Values&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Description&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;X-Amzn-SageMaker-Metadata-Stream-Supported&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Yes&lt;/strong&gt; (to opt in)&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;TRUE&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Signals that your model server supports the companion metadata stream. If absent or not &lt;code&gt;true&lt;/code&gt;, no metadata connection is attempted. Without the metadata stream, the session is not metered for the model provider’s software charge.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;2&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;X-Amzn-SageMaker-Metadata-Stream-Required&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;No&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;true&lt;/code&gt; / &lt;code&gt;false&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;This header only takes effect when &lt;code&gt;X-Amzn-SageMaker-Metadata-Stream-Supported&lt;/code&gt; is &lt;code&gt;true&lt;/code&gt;. If true, inference stops when the metadata connection fails. If false or absent, inference continues in data-only mode. Default: false. Choose true when metering continuity takes priority over inference availability. This setting doesn’t guarantee billing for malformed or dropped records.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;3&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;X-Amzn-SageMaker-Metadata-Invocation-Path&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;No&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Custom path string&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Overrides the default metadata WebSocket path. Default: &lt;code&gt;/invocations-bidirectional-stream-metadata&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-2-accept-the-metadata-websocket-connection"&gt;Step 2: Accept the metadata WebSocket connection&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;After your model server returns &lt;code&gt;X-Amzn-SageMaker-Metadata-Stream-Supported: true&lt;/code&gt;, SageMaker AI opens a second WebSocket connection to your model server at the default path &lt;code&gt;/invocations-bidirectional-stream-metadata&lt;/code&gt; (or the custom path you specified by using &lt;code&gt;X-Amzn-SageMaker-Metadata-Invocation-Path&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;SageMaker AI includes the header &lt;code&gt;X-Amzn-SageMaker-Request-Id&lt;/code&gt; on both the data and metadata WebSocket handshakes. Your model server must do the following:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Accept the WebSocket upgrade on the metadata path.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Use &lt;code&gt;X-Amzn-SageMaker-Request-Id&lt;/code&gt; to correlate the metadata connection with the corresponding data connection for the same inference session.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-3-send-metering-messages"&gt;Step 3: Send metering messages&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Send metering data as a JSON object in a WebSocket text frame on the metadata connection. Each frame must contain exactly one metering record. In this example, the product charges per second of audio, and the record reports 12 minutes of metered audio as 720 units:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
  "Metering": {
    "Dimension": "inference.count",
    "ConsumedUnits": 720,
    "ClientToken": "a3f2b1c0-unique-uuid"
  }
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Message fields:&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Field&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Required&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Type&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Constraints&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Description&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Metering&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Yes&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Object&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;—&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Container for the metering record.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;2&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Metering.Dimension&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Yes&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;String&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Must be &lt;code&gt;inference.count&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;The billing dimension. Must be &lt;code&gt;inference.count&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;3&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Metering.ConsumedUnits&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Yes&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Number&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;0 or greater&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;The units consumed since your previous record. Each record is billed separately. A value of 0 is accepted but not billed.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;4&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Metering.ClientToken&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;No&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;String&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Max 64 characters&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;A unique token (for example, a UUID) for idempotency. If the same &lt;code&gt;ClientToken&lt;/code&gt; is sent multiple times on the same connection, only the first occurrence is recorded.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Send metering records incrementally as usage accrues to reduce unreported usage if a session ends unexpectedly. Reconcile the reported quantities with AWS Marketplace reports and buyer charges to verify billing, including interrupted-session tests.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="constraints-and-limits"&gt;Constraints and limits&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Limit&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Value&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Notes&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Maximum message size&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;512 bytes&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Total size of the JSON text frame&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;2&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Maximum send rate&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1 message per second per connection&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Exceeding this rate might result in dropped messages&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;3&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Maximum Dimension length&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;128 characters&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;—&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;4&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Maximum ClientToken length&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;64 characters&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;—&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;5&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Messages per frame&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Exactly 1&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Don’t batch multiple metering records in a single frame&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;6&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Frame type&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Text frames only&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Binary frames are not supported for metering&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="error-handling-and-failure-modes"&gt;Error handling and failure modes&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Required header controls what happens when the metadata connection becomes unavailable. Set it to &lt;code&gt;true&lt;/code&gt; to stop inference, or use the default &lt;code&gt;false&lt;/code&gt; to let inference continue in data-only mode. This is a choice between metering continuity and inference availability. Malformed messages can still be dropped with either setting.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Scenario&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Required = true&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Required = false (default)&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Metadata WebSocket fails to connect&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Data stream is terminated&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Data stream continues normally (data-only mode)&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;2&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Metadata WebSocket drops mid-session&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Data stream is terminated&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Data stream continues normally. Metering stops&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;3&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Malformed metering message&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Message is dropped. Metadata connection stays open&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Message is dropped. Metadata connection stays open&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;4&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Data connection breaks mid-stream&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Metadata stream is closed&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Metadata stream is closed&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="list-your-model-on-aws-marketplace"&gt;List your model on AWS Marketplace&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Register your AWS account as an AWS Marketplace seller. For paid products, complete the tax and banking information.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Create a SageMaker AI model package from your container in a supported publishing Region, and an AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) role that AWS Marketplace can assume to read it.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In AWS Partner Central, choose &lt;strong&gt;Build&lt;/strong&gt;, &lt;strong&gt;Machine learning&lt;/strong&gt;, then &lt;strong&gt;Create machine learning product&lt;/strong&gt;, and add the model package as the first version.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose per-inference pricing. You set one per-unit price for real-time inference across all instance types, shown per request with up to three decimal places. You also set an hourly price for each batch transform instance type. Choose the pricing model when you create the listing because you can’t switch a released listing between hourly and per-inference pricing.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;After validation, request Public visibility. For enterprise buyers, extend private offers with a negotiated per-unit rate or a fixed-price contract.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="validate-billing-before-release"&gt;Validate billing before release&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Validate the integration with a &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/marketplace/latest/userguide/ml-product-lifecycle.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Limited listing&lt;/a&gt; and an allowlisted test buyer. Use an appropriate offer, including a private offer where needed, so the test exercises the intended unit and price mapping.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Run a known-quantity test. For a product priced per second of audio, send 12 minutes of metered audio and check that the reported records total 720 units.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Reconcile the model-server records with the seller’s usage reports and the buyer’s charges. Allow time for reports to populate and verify the usage type &lt;code&gt;&amp;lt;region code&amp;gt;-SMMP:inference.count.m.i.c&lt;/code&gt; (for example, &lt;code&gt;USE1-SMMP:inference.count.m.i.c&lt;/code&gt; in &lt;code&gt;us-east-1&lt;/code&gt;). Confirm the offer’s unit and price mapping rather than assuming a fixed reporting delay.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Test normal completion, interrupted sessions, metadata-stream loss, and malformed messages. Compare reported and billed quantities for each case with the SageMaker AI and AWS Marketplace teams before release.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Delete test endpoints when testing is complete to avoid ongoing infrastructure charges.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="getting-started-with-deepgram-on-aws-marketplace"&gt;Getting started with Deepgram on AWS Marketplace&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To deploy Deepgram with usage-based metering on SageMaker AI, follow these steps.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Subscribe to the Deepgram Voice AI streaming model you need on AWS Marketplace, such as &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/marketplace/pp/prodview-rwbcd44ekb2r6" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Deepgram Nova-3 Monolingual Speech-to-Text (STT) Streaming&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Deploy a SageMaker AI real-time endpoint using the Deepgram model package from your AWS Marketplace subscription.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Connect to the endpoint using the SageMaker AI bidirectional streaming API and stream audio through its HTTP/2 event stream.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Deepgram’s model server calculates metering automatically, and Amazon SageMaker AI and AWS Marketplace process it. You don’t need to configure anything on the client side.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When you finish testing, delete the SageMaker AI real-time endpoint and model resource to stop infrastructure charges. If you no longer need the product, remove the AWS Marketplace subscription.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With usage-based metering for bidirectional streaming, model providers can align AWS Marketplace software pricing with reported usage during a live session. Customers pay the software charge for the metered units defined by the product and offer. Amazon SageMaker AI endpoint infrastructure charges remain separate and still apply. Learn more about &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/sagemaker-ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon SageMaker AI&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;For customers, subscribe to a Deepgram Voice AI streaming model on AWS Marketplace, such as &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/marketplace/pp/prodview-rwbcd44ekb2r6" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Deepgram Nova-3 Monolingual Speech-to-Text (STT) Streaming&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;For model providers, follow the integration walkthrough in this post to add usage-based metering to your own model server.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Introducing Kimi K3.1 Pro on Amazon Bedrock</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/introducing-kimi-k3-1-pro-on-amazon-bedrock/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Aris Tsakpinis]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 11 Nov 2026 01:07:19 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Announcements]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Foundational (100)]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">a1657d26dd7d2a16f20836a0d0a5e765a3ee4064</guid>

					<description>Kimi K3.1 Pro from Moonshot AI is now available on Amazon Bedrock. Learn how to get started through the OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions, Responses, and Converse APIs, optimize inference with explicit prompt caching, and use the model as a coding assistant in OpenCode.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed from &lt;a href="https://www.kimi.ai/blog/kimi-k3-1-pro" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Moonshot AI&lt;/a&gt; is now available on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;. Part of Moonshot AI’s new K3.1 series, it’s a post-trained version of Kimi K3 with the same 3T-class open-weight architecture and 1-million-token context window, trained with substantially more post-training compute. According to Moonshot AI, that extra training targets long-horizon agentic work and fewer factual errors. It also improves professional tasks such as financial modeling and office documents, and coding for game engines, 3D scenes, and computer-aided design (CAD) scripts. Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed generates output tokens at a higher rate and Moonshot AI reports 2–3 times the speed of Kimi K3.1 Pro.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed uses the same endpoint, APIs, and features as Kimi K3 on Amazon Bedrock. If you already build with Kimi K3, you can try it by changing the model ID in your existing code.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed supports tool calling, structured output, configurable reasoning, image input, and streaming. You can adopt Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed without changing your security posture. Your data is processed within the AWS data boundary, isn’t shared with Moonshot AI, and isn’t used to train the model. Zero data retention is always enabled for inference requests, and with zero operator access, AWS operators can’t view your prompts and completions during inference.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we show you how to get started with Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed on Amazon Bedrock. We also cover how to lower costs with the Flex tier, explicit prompt caching for repeated context, and how to use the model as a coding assistant in OpenCode.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="get-started-with-kimi-k3.1-pro-highspeed-on-amazon-bedrock"&gt;Get started with Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed on Amazon Bedrock&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To try the model without writing code, open the &lt;a href="https://console.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock console&lt;/a&gt;, choose &lt;strong&gt;Test&lt;/strong&gt;, then &lt;strong&gt;Playground&lt;/strong&gt;, and select &lt;strong&gt;Kimi K3.1&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;From code, Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed is available on the &lt;code&gt;bedrock-runtime&lt;/code&gt; endpoint through the OpenAI-compatible &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/inference-chat-completions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Chat Completions&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/inference-responses-api.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Responses&lt;/a&gt; APIs, and through the Amazon Bedrock &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/conversation-inference.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Converse&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/inference-invoke.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Invoke&lt;/a&gt; APIs. We recommend the Chat Completions API. You reach the model through cross-Region inference profiles:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;global.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1&lt;/code&gt; routes requests to any supported commercial AWS Region, and costs less per token than the geographic profiles. We recommend it for most workloads.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;us.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1&lt;/code&gt; keeps processing within US Regions, and &lt;code&gt;in.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1&lt;/code&gt; keeps it within India Regions, for workloads with data residency requirements.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To follow the examples, you need:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An AWS account with access to Amazon Bedrock.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Python 3.11 or later, with the &lt;a href="https://pypi.org/project/openai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;OpenAI SDK&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://pypi.org/project/aws-bedrock-token-generator/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;aws-bedrock-token-generator&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href="https://pypi.org/project/boto3/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;boto3&lt;/a&gt; installed (&lt;code&gt;pip install -U openai aws-bedrock-token-generator boto3&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/iam/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM)&lt;/a&gt; identity allowed to call &lt;code&gt;bedrock:InvokeModel&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;bedrock:InvokeModelWithResponseStream&lt;/code&gt; on the Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed inference profiles. Add &lt;code&gt;bedrock:CallWithBearerToken&lt;/code&gt; if you authenticate with a bearer token.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="call-kimi-k3.1-pro-with-the-chat-completions-api"&gt;Call Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed with the Chat Completions API&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If your application already uses the OpenAI SDK, point it at the Amazon Bedrock endpoint and change the model name. The following example creates a short-term bearer token from your AWS credentials and sends one request:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;from openai import OpenAI
from aws_bedrock_token_generator import provide_token

REGION = "us-west-2"
MODEL_ID = "global.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1"

# A short-term token derived from your AWS credentials, so no long-lived key is stored in code
client = OpenAI(
    base_url=f"https://bedrock-runtime.{REGION}.amazonaws.com/openai/v1",
    api_key=provide_token(region=REGION),
)

completion = client.chat.completions.create(
    model=MODEL_ID,
    messages=[
        {"role": "system", "content": "You are a concise senior engineer."},
        {"role": "user", "content": "When should I prefer a queue over a stream?"},
    ],
    reasoning_effort="high",
)
print(completion.choices[0].message.content)&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed always reasons before it answers. With &lt;code&gt;reasoning_effort&lt;/code&gt;, you control how much: &lt;code&gt;low&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;high&lt;/code&gt;, or &lt;code&gt;max&lt;/code&gt;. If you don’t set it, the model uses &lt;code&gt;max&lt;/code&gt;, so a lower value is the most direct way to reduce latency and output tokens on simpler tasks.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="call-kimi-k3.1-pro-highspeed-with-the-responses-api"&gt;Call Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed with the Responses API&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The same client works with the Responses API, which uses &lt;code&gt;reasoning.effort&lt;/code&gt; for the same setting:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;response = client.responses.create(
    model=MODEL_ID,
    input="Summarize the trade-offs between optimistic and pessimistic locking.",
    reasoning={"effort": "low"},
)
print(response.output_text)&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="call-kimi-k3.1-pro-highspeed-with-the-converse-api"&gt;Call Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed with the Converse API&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If you already use the Amazon Bedrock Converse API, the change is the model ID:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;import boto3

bedrock = boto3.client("bedrock-runtime", region_name="us-west-2")
response = bedrock.converse(
    modelId="global.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1",
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": [{"text": "Explain idempotency keys in two sentences."}]}],
)

# The model can return a reasoning block before the answer, so pick the text block
content = response["output"]["message"]["content"]
print(next(block["text"] for block in content if "text" in block))&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="Lower-costs-with-the-Flex-tier"&gt;Lower costs with the Flex tier&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/service-tiers-inference.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Flex tier&lt;/a&gt; serves Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed at half the Standard price, in exchange for potentially longer response times. It’s well suited to workloads where cost efficiency is a higher priority than response time, such as model evaluations, content summarization, and background tasks in agentic workflows. Because you choose the tier for each request, one application can mix tiers. For example, the same application could use Priority for latency-critical requests, Standard for everyday traffic, and Flex for cost-sensitive work. Your on-demand quota is shared across the Standard, Priority, and Flex tiers.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To use Flex, set &lt;code&gt;service_tier&lt;/code&gt; to &lt;code&gt;flex&lt;/code&gt;. The following example reuses the Chat Completions client from earlier:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;completion = client.chat.completions.create(
    model=MODEL_ID,
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "List edge cases to test in a function that parses ISO 8601 dates."}],
    service_tier="flex",
)
print(completion.choices[0].message.content)
print(completion.service_tier)  # The tier that served the request&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;On the Converse API, set &lt;code&gt;serviceTier&lt;/code&gt; to &lt;code&gt;{"type": "flex"}&lt;/code&gt; instead. To see how much of your traffic each tier served, use the &lt;code&gt;ServiceTier&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;ResolvedServiceTier&lt;/code&gt; dimensions in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cloudwatch/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon CloudWatch&lt;/a&gt;. We recommend starting with a small share of your traffic on Flex and comparing latency and cost before you move more.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="optimize-inference-with-explicit-prompt-caching"&gt;Optimize inference with explicit prompt caching&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Agents and coding assistants resend the same large prefix on every turn: system instructions, tool definitions, repository conventions, and reference documents. Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed caches repeated prefixes automatically, and with explicit prompt caching, you can mark where the stable prefix ends to improve your cache hit rate. Later requests reuse that prefix instead of processing it again, which lowers both latency and input cost for long, repeated context.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A few rules apply:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Explicit caching works on the Chat Completions, Responses, and Invoke APIs, but not on Converse.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A &lt;code&gt;prompt_cache_breakpoint&lt;/code&gt; must follow at least 256 tokens of stable content.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Cached content is retained for at least 30 minutes.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Cache writes are billed at a higher rate than standard input. Cache reads are billed at a discounted rate and don’t count toward your input tokens-per-minute quota.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following example caches a long system prompt and adds a second breakpoint after the user content, so a follow-up question on the same document can reuse both layers:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;from pathlib import Path

LONG_STATIC_PROMPT = Path("repo_guidelines.md").read_text()  # stable content, at least 256 tokens
QUESTION = "Which of these guidelines apply to database migrations?"

def cached_text(text):
    # Marks the end of a cacheable prefix
    return {"type": "input_text", "text": text, "prompt_cache_breakpoint": {"mode": "explicit"}}

response = client.responses.create(
    model=MODEL_ID,
    extra_body={"prompt_cache_options": {"mode": "explicit"}},
    input=[
        {"type": "message", "role": "system", "content": [cached_text(LONG_STATIC_PROMPT)]},
        {"type": "message", "role": "user", "content": [cached_text(QUESTION)]},
    ],
)
details = response.usage.input_tokens_details
print(f"Cache read: {details.cached_tokens} tokens")  # greater than 0 on a cache hit&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For more information, refer to &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/prompt-caching.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Prompt caching for faster model inference&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="kimi-k3.1-pro-in-practice-coding-with-opencode"&gt;Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed in practice: Coding with OpenCode&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://opencode.ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;OpenCode&lt;/a&gt; is an open source, model-agnostic coding agent that runs in your terminal. You can connect it to Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed through the Amazon Bedrock Chat Completions API, using OpenCode’s &lt;a href="https://opencode.ai/docs/providers/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;OpenAI-compatible provider&lt;/a&gt; and an &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/api-keys.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock API key&lt;/a&gt;. To create a key, open the Amazon Bedrock console in the AWS Region that you set in &lt;code&gt;baseURL&lt;/code&gt;, then choose &lt;strong&gt;API keys&lt;/strong&gt;. Short-term keys last up to 12 hours, work only in the Region where you generate them, and are recommended for production use. Long-term keys are intended for exploration.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Add the following to &lt;code&gt;~/.config/opencode/opencode.json&lt;/code&gt;, replacing the Region in &lt;code&gt;baseURL&lt;/code&gt; with your own:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
  "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
  "providers": {
    "amazon-bedrock-chat-completions": {
      "name": "Amazon Bedrock (Chat Completions)",
      "package": "@opencode/ai/providers/openai-compatible",
      "settings": {
        "baseURL": "https://bedrock-runtime.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/openai/v1"
      },
      "models": {
        "global.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1": {
          "name": "Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed",
          "capabilities": {
            "reasoning": { "supported": true, "effort": ["low", "high", "max"] }
          }
        }
      }
    }
  },
  "model": "amazon-bedrock-chat-completions/global.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1"
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Start OpenCode in your project directory, enter &lt;code&gt;/connect&lt;/code&gt;, choose &lt;strong&gt;Amazon Bedrock (Chat Completions)&lt;/strong&gt;, and paste your API key as shown in Figure 1. OpenCode stores the key, so you connect once per key. To switch between Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed and other models during a session, enter &lt;code&gt;/models&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 442px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ml-22229-opencode-connect-dialog.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ml-22229-opencode-connect-dialog.png" alt="Connecting OpenCode to the Amazon Bedrock (Chat Completions) provider"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Connecting OpenCode to the Amazon Bedrock (Chat Completions) provider&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;code&gt;capabilities&lt;/code&gt; entry in the configuration lists the reasoning effort levels that the model supports, so OpenCode offers &lt;code&gt;low&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;high&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;max&lt;/code&gt; as variants of the model, as shown in Figure 2:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 589px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ml-22229-opencode-select-variant.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/11/ml-22229-opencode-select-variant.png" alt="Reasoning effort variants for Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed in OpenCode"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: Reasoning effort variants for Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed in OpenCode&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Output speed matters most in agentic coding. To complete a single task, a coding agent makes many model calls in a row. It reads files, plans a change, edits code, runs tests, and fixes what fails. Each call has to finish before the next one starts, and much of that time is spent generating reasoning and code. A higher output-token rate shortens every step, so the whole edit-test-fix loop finishes sooner and you wait less between iterations.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="availability"&gt;Availability&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed is available on Amazon Bedrock through the &lt;code&gt;global.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;us.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;in.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1&lt;/code&gt; cross-Region inference profiles. For supported Regions and model details, refer to the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/model-card-moonshot-ai-kimi-k3-1-highspeed.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed model card&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/models-region-compatibility.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Regional availability&lt;/a&gt;. For pricing, refer to &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock pricing&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started, try Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed on the &lt;a href="https://console.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock console&lt;/a&gt;. To discuss your use case with the Amazon Bedrock team, &lt;a href="https://pages.awscloud.com/Amazon-Bedrock-Contact-Us.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;connect with us&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
